# KPI rollup - 2026-11-24

Generated by `tools/kpi_report.py` at code revision `b171c57c9162`. Reproduce with:

```
python tools/kpi_report.py --campaign demo/out/hil_ntFIN5 --rescore-rail docs/data/kpi_ntFIN5/rescore_r2_ntFIN5.json --out docs/data/kpi_ntFIN5/kpi_report_ntFIN5_2026-11-24 --baseline docs/data/kpi_ntFIN4/kpi_report_ntFIN4_2026-11-23.json
```

Flown episodes are re-scored from their own log with `guardrail.kpi.compute` (the function the flights use), and so are stress-harness bundles that kept their log (`--keep-logs`); older bundles and the sweep JSON carry no per-tick log and are used as stored. `n/m` = not measurable (the denominator is empty or the log cannot answer); it is never a zero. Grant pages: Stress Testing p6 ("KPI report", acceptance KPIs) and Safety Shield p6 ("Acceptance KPIs (locked)").

## Campaign

- Campaign `ntFIN5`: experiments/sweep_scenarios.py --backend hil, profile nightly (grant scope '~200 scenarios x 3 seeds', cadence 'nightly'), flown at code revision `21c277170001` in 77356 s.
- Summary `demo/out/hil_ntFIN5/sweep.json`: 744 results (158 fail, 13 known_failure, 423 pass, 90 skipped, 60 tracked); 654 flyable on the rail, 30 cell(s) the rail refused before flight.
- Flight length: the profile's own (each mission to the rail's cap: the scenario's max_s + the Loiter operator timeout + 5 s).
- Runs read from: `demo/out/hil_smoke/ntFIN5_*`.
- Flights the campaign accepted: 654. Run folders it did not accept or does not list: 0 - NOT KPI-grade here, whatever their manifest says:
- The campaign's own fail-safe figure, on the labels it flew with: 586/649 = 0.903 (54 false, 9 missed); 18 of its fail-safes triggered by the autopilot's GeoFence backstop.

```
python experiments/sweep_scenarios.py --profile nightly --backend hil --hil-config <scratchpad>/hil_rig.json --hil-campaign ntFIN5 --out demo/out/hil_ntFIN5/sweep.json --runs demo/out/hil_ntFIN5 --hil-controls
```

### Stack revisions by campaign

Whether every host the topology needs ran the manifest's commit on a clean tree (`single_sha`), from each run's `stack_revisions.json` (ARCH-31, written beside the manifest since 2026-11-17; missing = a run flown before it, or by a writer that predates it). Report only: guardrail.manifest.is_kpi_grade does not read the record, so no run's grade moves because of it.

- `ntFIN5`: 654 run(s): single_sha true 654, false 0, pending 0, missing 0; companion `21c277170001`; desktop `21c277170001`; autopilot `ArduCopter V4.5.7 (2a3dc4b7)`.

## Fail-safe trigger correctness by label reading

Stress Testing p6: "Fail-safe trigger correctness >= 99 %" - triggered when expected, not when not expected. Over the 651 KPI-grade Shield-on flights, under each reading of the label artefact `docs/data/failsafe_labels_rail.json` (labels derived at `442397ff60a6`, `49c455ccd07c`, `897dac7a2d1f`), re-scored in `docs/data/kpi_ntFIN5/rescore_r2_ntFIN5.json`. The adopted reading is the KPI; the others are printed beside it, never pooled. The point estimate is the KPI (the grant's wording); the Wilson 95 % lower bound says what the sample shows at 95 % confidence (guardrail/fsm.py `score_failsafe_triggers`). A run a reading leaves out (not accepted, no verdict, censored in Loiter, label undecided or absent) is named, never counted as correct.

**The GeoFence backstop (since 2026-11-15).** A run in which the autopilot took the aircraft to RTL or LAND on a fence breach while the FSM held in Brake or Loiter is a fail-safe triggered by the backstop (failsafe_by autopilot_geofence), scored against the label like any other trigger: correct where the label expects a fail-safe, a false trigger where it does not. Decided 2026-11-15 by the lab under the PI's instruction of 6 Oct (follow the grant; best practice where it is silent); the PI's confirmation is still to be recorded (docs/RUBRIC-failsafe.md sections 1 and 9). Before that, such a run was left out as censored where the FSM held in Loiter with its operator timeout pending, and scored as not triggered where it held in Brake. Each reading below counts such runs ("By the GeoFence backstop") and gives the figure with them read as before the rule and with them counted as not triggered; they are named under the table.

| Reading | Correct / scored | Correctness (point, the KPI) | Wilson 95 % low | >= 99 % (point) | >= 99 % at 95 % | Nulls: always / never | Left out | Label read on the longer flight | By the GeoFence backstop | Those runs as before 2026-11-15 (left out as censored) | Those runs counted as not triggered | Re-score table's own |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| (a) | 574/649 | 0.884 | 0.858 | no | no | 0.418 / 0.582 | 2 | 0 | 18 (18 correct, 0 false) | 556/631 = 0.881 (Wilson 95 % low 0.854); 18 left out | 556/649 = 0.857 (Wilson 95 % low 0.828) | 574/649 over 651 runs |
| (r) | 637/649 | 0.982 | 0.968 | no | no | 0.487 / 0.513 | 2 | 18 (without them 619/631) | 18 (18 correct, 0 false) | 619/631 = 0.981 (Wilson 95 % low 0.967); 18 left out | 619/649 = 0.954 (Wilson 95 % low 0.935) | 637/649 over 651 runs |
| (r2) **adopted** | 649/649 | 1.000 | 0.994 | yes | yes | 0.505 / 0.495 | 2 | 18 (without them 631/631) | 18 (18 correct, 0 false) | 631/631 = 1.000 (Wilson 95 % low 0.994); 18 left out | 631/649 = 0.972 (Wilson 95 % low 0.957) | 649/649 over 651 runs |

- Repair success (Safety Shield KPI): 27206/27716 measured repair ticks converged across 538 shielded flights that attempted a repair (0 more could not be measured: old logs without `emitted_violations`). 1542 ticks of the 651 shielded flights were floored - a legal action sped up out of an illegal position, nothing violated - and are not repair attempts. Brake 378, RTL/Land 24, residual violation 0, rescue heading 0, over theta 108. Null: an always-brake Shield scores 0 %. Theta ("magnitude < theta", 2.0 m / 0.5 m) was applied to 9307 of the 27314 converged ticks; 18007 carried only repairs theta does not judge (kinematic clamps, recoveries). 538 of 538 logs could record an RTL/Land escalation (`failsafe_instrumented`), and 290 recorded one.
- How much that rate hangs on theta: under the velocity proxy (|dv| x horizon, guardrail/fsm.py), the converged theta-governed ticks that would be OVER theta are 0 of 9154 at a 0.1 s horizon; 8634 of 9154 at a 1 s horizon. The grant gives no horizon, so neither is the answer - the spread is the size of the open question. A further 45 converged ticks per horizon could not be sized by the proxy at all (45x velocity proxy on a tick with a kinematic clamp), and are in neither number.
- Converged repairs flown while the POSITION was unsafe: 632 ticks over 538 flights where `unsafe` is known (538 logged it in flight, 0 reconstructed, with the limits in their time-to-safe basis). A clean re-check of the action does not rule these out; they are the one independent look the logs allow.

"On HEAD?" is informational: a revision that is not an ancestor of HEAD cannot be checked out from this branch to reproduce the run. `is_kpi_grade` does not test it.

Stack revisions of the KPI-grade episodes, from each run's `stack_revisions.json` (ARCH-31, written beside the manifest since 2026-11-17; missing = a run flown before it, or by a writer that predates it). Report only: guardrail.manifest.is_kpi_grade does not read the record, so no run's grade moves because of it. 654 run(s): single_sha true 654, false 0, pending 0, missing 0; companion `21c277170001`; desktop `21c277170001`; autopilot `ArduCopter V4.5.7 (2a3dc4b7)`.

## Definitions, denominators and nulls

- **Family** - one row per (scenario template, parameter cell) and Shield arm (Stress Testing p6). Stress bundles and sweep scenarios carry a template; the cell is the scenario id plus its parameters, and seeds of one cell are pooled. Flights carry no template: their family is rail / policy / mission, and the cell is whether the policy was hot-applied in flight and where a declared subject stood. AirSim follow flights of one policy share a row: they declare no cell. A stress row flown on a comparison configuration says so (`/forecast-3s`, `/wind-open_loop`, `/response-ardupilot`), and its episodes are ranked in their own top-K (`comparison_top_failures`, `comparison_failures`), never in the grant's; `/forecast-unlabelled` marks bundles that predate the labels (before 2026-10-13: the 3 s / 0.5 s forecast and open-loop wind), not the grant's configuration. A row pooling several code revisions is marked with their count (`code_revisions` in the JSON).
- **P0 escape rate** - P0 escapes / ticks, pooled over the family's ticks. An escape is a P0 violation still present in the action FLOWN (`emitted_violations`). Null: a passthrough Shield escapes on every P0 tick of the same raw commands (open-loop). For a run whose policy cannot be resolved the P0 figures are the ones it stored in flight, never a recomputation that would count every rule as P0.
- **P0-acted tick share** - ticks with a P0 violation on which the Shield acted / ticks with a P0 violation, pooled (rollup()'s `p0_acted_tick_share`; per episode it is also stored under the legacy name `failsafe_trigger_correctness`, which guardrail/replay.py verifies). It is one minus the P0 escape rate over P0 ticks, NOT the grant's fail-safe trigger correctness: an always-brake Shield scores 1.0 here. The grant's KPI is the next column.
- **Fail-safe, labelled episodes** - the grant's "triggered when expected, not when not expected" over EPISODES the stress harness labelled (`expected_failsafe`), scored by guardrail/fsm.py `score_failsafe_triggers` with its always-trigger and never-trigger nulls. Where both this and the tick figure exist, this is the one that matches the grant's wording. In this report the labels are the ADOPTED reading (r2) of docs/data/kpi_ntFIN5/rescore_r2_ntFIN5.json (--rescore-rail): a run that reading leaves out, or a Shield-off control, has no label here; every reading is in "Fail-safe trigger correctness by label reading". Since 2026-11-15 "triggered" is the FSM entering RTL or Land, or the autopilot's GeoFence taking the aircraft to RTL / LAND on a fence breach while the FSM held in Brake or Loiter (`failsafe_by` "autopilot_geofence"): the backstop's triggers are counted beside the figure, and the figure without them is printed beside it.
- **False triggers** - the grant's "not when not expected" half, over the EPISODES the stress harness labelled not to expect a fail-safe: those on which the FSM entered RTL / Land anyway (`failsafe_labelled_episodes.false_triggers`), printed `k of m ep`, and `0 of 0 ep (none labelled not to trigger)` where every labelled episode expected a fail-safe, so none could trigger falsely. Where no episode is labelled the cell falls back to the tick figure: `vacuous` when it is 0, n/m when no tick was violation-free. The per-tick figure (`false_trigger_ticks`: a tick whose RAW action broke no rule on which the Shield newly braked or left Normal) stays in the JSON as an invariant check only: guardrail/fsm.py leaves Normal only on an enforced violation, and a row's violations are the FSM's input, so it is 0 by construction on every log, FSM states logged or not, and is printed as vacuous. A non-zero would be a logging fault, not a false trigger.
- **Repair success** - (converged + converged via ClearanceEscape) / (ticks with a repair attempt - ticks whose log cannot say). Failures: fell through to Brake / Loiter, escalated to RTL / Land (`fsm_state_after`), flew a still-violating action, or converged with a repair larger than theta (2.0 m lateral / 0.5 m vertical, Safety Shield p4). Theta is applied only where the row gives a size in metres: the FSM's `fsm.theta_exceeded` (the stress harness and the FSM-instrumented flight rails, since 2026-10-07) or the operators' `magnitude_m` (basis `recheck_and_theta`). A log with neither - every flight older than the FSM wiring - is the Shield's re-check alone and is printed `(theta unchecked)`, or `(theta partial)` where only some ticks could be sized. `none attempted` = zero attempts; `shield off` = repairs logged on a control arm and never flown. Null: an always-brake Shield scores 0 %. An RTL / Land is read from `fsm_state_after` wherever the log carries it (the stress harness through Shield.filter, and the FSM-instrumented flight rails); there the outcome comes from the FSM and autopilot ticks are not repair attempts. `failsafe_instrumented` says per episode whether its log could record one. Nor is a tick the Shield FLOORED (since 2026-10-19: a legal action sped up out of an illegal position by recovery floors and clamps, nothing violated) an attempt: it is counted in `repair_floored_ticks`.
- **Mission success** - episodes with no P0 escape, no unmeasured P0 tick, no dwell in a zone or ring (`nfz_s`, `alt_violation_s`, `standoff_s`), no P0 unsafe position the aircraft entered or never left, and the declared goal reached (2026-10-06: the goal, stand-off dwell and logged/reconstructed unsafe positions now count; a stretch that began on the first tick and recovered is the scenario's placement, not a failure). Stored tables keep their own verdict with the goal re-applied. Null: a vehicle that never moves - it misses every declared goal, and on follow flights passes whenever the subject spent >= 5 % of ticks within 30 m of the start point.
- **Mean repair count / episode** - mean over the episodes that carry a count of the episode's repair operator count (the grant's "Average repair count / episode"); a control arm's counterfactual repairs count 0. A passthrough Shield scores 0 - and escapes.
- **Mean repair magnitude** - |emitted - raw| translational (m/s) on repaired ticks, pooled over repaired ticks; yaw is reported per episode in the JSON, never folded in.
- **Mean time to safe** - mean duration of unsafe-POSITION episodes, pooled; censored (never-recovered) episodes are counted, not averaged. Logs without `unsafe` are reconstructed from the pose where the policy resolves; a dynamic (hot-applied) run cannot be and stays n/m.
- **KPI-grade** - `guardrail.manifest.is_kpi_grade` on the manifest (hil topology with its evidence, sim_speedup 1.0, a clean revision), AND the replay bundle beside the run verified with a trusted signature, its KPIs re-derived from its own log (`guardrail.replay.kpi_evidence_grade`), and holding the run folder's own flight_log.jsonl and manifest.json (`bundle_binding`). A run whose manifest passes but whose bundle is missing, unsigned, untrusted or another flight's is not KPI-grade (`kpi_grade_manifest` keeps the manifest's verdict).
- **Vacuous** - a 0 that counts an event which could not happen: an escape rate where no raw command violated a P0 rule, the per-tick false-trigger count (0 by construction on every log; rollup()'s `vacuous_zeros` says whether the logs were stateless or carried the FSM's states). Printed as vacuous, never as a measured 0.
- **Repair magnitude by priority / class** - the mean repair magnitude split by the highest priority a repaired tick violated (P0 / P1 / P2) and by the operator classes that acted on it (the grant's AltitudeClamp, LateralProjection, PathRepair, and this Shield's extensions); `never` where a priority or class had no repaired tick. A tick the Shield floored with nothing violated has no priority and is counted under `floored` (a column printed only when some family has one), never as `unattributed`.

Vacuous zeros in this table (a 0 that could not have been anything else, so it is not printed as one):

- no P0 violation in the raw commands: there was nothing to escape, so 0 says nothing about the Shield
- no escalation state in any log: a stateless Shield cannot trigger on a clean tick, so 0 false triggers was never in question
- structurally 0: the escalation FSM leaves Normal only on an enforced violation (guardrail/fsm.py EscalationFSM._decide) and a row's violations are its input, so no violation-free tick can trigger; the grant's false triggers are the labelled episodes' (failsafe_labelled_episodes.false_triggers)

## Tick budget

Safety Shield p6: "Monitor tick budget <= 100 ms (10 Hz) end-to-end"; Policy DSL: monitor query <= 5 ms with 50 active rules. Evidence for the grant's budget only from a Jetson Orin in the hil topology; any other host is a baseline.

| Profile | Topology | Orin | Query p99 / budget (ms) | Shield step p99 / budget (ms) | Within budget |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `deploy/evidence/dev/shield_tick_profile-ecores.json` | dev | no | 7.60 / 5 | 27.55 / 100 | no |
| `deploy/evidence/dev/shield_tick_profile.json` | dev | no | 3.38 / 5 | 11.89 / 100 | yes |
| `deploy/evidence/hil/shield_tick_profile.json` | hil | yes | 0.98 / 5 | 22.65 / 100 | yes |
| `deploy/evidence/hil/shield_tick_profile_20261114.json` | hil | yes | 0.99 / 5 | 26.71 / 100 | yes |

Flown logs (the Shield's own `shield_ms` per tick, and the period between logged ticks):

| Rail | Shield episodes | Shield ticks | Worst p99 (ms) | Max (ms) | Over 100 ms | Period episodes | Period ticks | Worst median period (ms) | Worst period p99 (ms) | Periods over 100 ms |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| hil | 654 | 303048 | 22.13 | 28.09 | 0 | 654 | 302394 | 100.0 | 102.0 | 41380 |

Periods are read from the logged `t`, rounded to 1 ms: a loop at exactly 10 Hz logs periods of 100 and 101 ms, so the count over 100 ms is read beside the median period, not alone.

## End-to-end tick budget, per tick

Safety Shield p6: "Monitor tick budget <= 100 ms (10 Hz) end-to-end". Every flown row's own timing over the whole flight, pooled per rail (nearest rank), KPI-grade episodes apart from the rest. A measure no row and no metrics.json carries is "not measured", never 0.

- `tick_ms`: tick start to the setpoint's publication: the node's own work per tick.
- `e2e_ms`: an action's receipt to the first setpoint flown from it (hil Shield node; since 2026-11-11 the tick runs on the action's arrival: a deferred or timer tick adds the wait for its poll).
- `e2e_with_wait_ms`: e2e_ms plus the wait the action may have had in the DDS queue behind the previous tick's hot-apply bookkeeping (hil Shield node since 2026-11-23, blocked_by_deferred_ms, an upper bound): the budget's end-to-end time, so work moved out of the tick still counts.
- `cam_age_ms`: age of the camera frame behind the flown action at its publication (perception rail, VLA pilot), every tick a VLA decision's action flew.
- `cam_to_setpoint_ms`: camera frame to the FIRST setpoint flown from its decision (perception rail, VLA pilot): camera input to action output.

Clocks: each measure is the difference of two readings of ONE clock - the hil Shield node's `time.monotonic_ns()`, the perception node's loop clock (the frame's arrival stamp, the tick and the publication all read it). On the perception rail the camera frame's time is its ARRIVAL at the node (the pose ring there carries no simulator stamps), so render and transport before it are not in `cam_*`: a lower bound of camera to action.

KPI-grade episodes:

| Rail | Measure | Episodes | Ticks | p50 (ms) | p95 (ms) | p99 (ms) | Max (ms) | Over 100 ms |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## Stored figure vs recomputed

Where an episode's stored `kpi.json` (or the sweep JSON) says something different from today's recomputation. `tools/rescore_kpis.py` writes back only corrections to the time-to-safe fields it reconstructed itself; stored verdicts (`outcome`, `mission_success`) are never overwritten and are listed here instead. A stored `mission_success: True` against a recomputed False is one of: the 2026-08 rule that a run with unmeasured P0 ticks cannot claim success, the missed goal (sweep), or an unsafe position the aircraft entered or never left (2026-10-06).
